qMES: The high model lengthscales (up to 4854.527) suggest potential overestimation of smoothness in some dimensions, leading to uncertainty about the true landscape. qMES (Max-value Entropy Search) is suitable here as it focuses on reducing uncertainty about the location of the global minimum, prioritizing exploration in regions that could potentially yield the best improvement. This aligns with the need to break the current f_min stagnation and address the model's possible overconfidence in certain areas.
qPES: The extremely high model lengthscales (up to 4750.471) indicate potential overestimation of smoothness in some dimensions, creating uncertainty about the true landscape's structure. qPES (Predictive Entropy Search) is chosen here to prioritize exploration in regions with high predictive uncertainty, aiming to reduce overall entropy and identify areas likely to contain the global minimum. This aligns with the need to address the model's inflated lengthscale estimates and break the f_min stagnation through targeted exploration of under-explored yet promising regions.
qJES: The extreme lengthscales (up to 6410.963) suggest the model overestimates smoothness in some dimensions, creating regions of spurious confidence. qJES (Joint Entropy Search) is selected to jointly optimize exploration and exploitation by targeting areas where both predictive uncertainty and uncertainty about the global optimum are high. This addresses the model's inflated lengthscale estimates and the stagnation at f_min by prioritizing regions that could simultaneously reduce uncertainty and yield potential improvements.
